# TASKS — the job list. Tick `[x]` only when written, tested, committed (name the test).

Tj's request, 2026-11-02 (verbatim): "In the launcher repo, I will be building an Android 10 go launcher apk. I want
it to use github actions for as much as it can contribute, and save progress and checkpoints to github. Set up the
foundation for the launcher repo. I want there to be a comprehensive checkpoint, ladder, and save system so that if
Claude is interrupted by usage, another session can pick up the work without loss of progress. Look at the other
repos and their checkpoint systems for this purpose and adopt the best system but modify it for the launcher repo.
Install and use any skills and plug-ins needed to help this project. Install or adopt the tools needed to build apks
with github actions, just like the other repos do."

## Features — Tj's request, 2026-11-02 (second message, verbatim summary of the spec; screenshots saved in research/uploads/)
Device: Secure generic Alps 711 tb8168p1_bsp ("About tablet: 711"), MT8168B, Mali-G52 MC2, modified Android 10 Go (API 29),
security patch 2021-07-05, fingerprint alps/vnd_tb8168p1_bsp/tb8168p1_bsp:10/QP1A.190711.020/1719850017:user/release-keys,
Treble, kernel 4.14.141, secure boot, LOCKED bootloader, build sys_mssi_t_32_ago-user.2024070111, custom build alps-mp-q0.mp3-V4.160,
stock launcher Quickstep v10, 600x1024 screen, 3-button nav, storage 96% used (1.32 GB free), 32-bit userspace (mssi_t_32).
Tj: "Build a custom launcher and send it to me as an apk file", default launcher + boots with Android, identical in look/function to
stock Quickstep 10 (may adopt the Quickstep code; Claude may download and analyse it), default Android 10 Go layouts/fonts/app drawer,
screenshots attached (home grid 3 cols, folders Connect/Games, long-press popup Home settings / Wallpapers, Home settings page,
quick settings with only Auto-rotate tile). Modifications:

## Tj's answers, 2026-11-02 (verbatim)
1) "I can send adb commands at a later time, but I want as much as possible to work without adb, with an option to add adb features later"
2) "I want a two finger clockwise gesture to return to quickstep, and either a small killswitch button on screen or a two finger counter clockwise gesture for the kill switch, or pressing both volume buttons at the same time"

keystore committed under signing/ (Tj waived secrecy), workflow signs with it.

## Tj, 2026-11-02 (after v0.2.3), verbatim summary
"Now build the ability to hide apps from the app drawer. Any hidden apps should never show up in the app drawer search bar. Additionally, newly installed apps are automatically hidden from the app drawer, unless i go into the launcher settings and explicitly unhide apps one by one. The settings menu for the custom launcher should only be visible and accessible when the quickstep launcher is on. The goal is to 'lock' the user into the custom launcher, which looks and behaves completely stock, unless i do the gesture to switch to quickstep.
Then consider how you can build a cloned settings app which looks and behaves exactly identical to the stock Android 10 go settings ... I can customize it to remove settings and rearrange settings. Maybe download the actual settings app code just like you did with the actual quickstep launcher code?
Then consider even faster ways to hide the screenshot button. Can the accessibility mode prevent the power menu completely without hiding it? Can the accessibility feature make the alerted power menu show immediately on side key press (such as the time it usually takes for a quick button press to turn the screen off) while the original Android power menu takes a little longer because it requires a long side key press?"
- [x] L2/L6 HIDE1: hidden apps never in drawer or drawer search; apps installed after the baseline are hidden until Tj allows them one by one (chooser in Launcher control)  [DONE or superseded: see STATUS SNAPSHOT]
- [x] LOCK1 (v0.3.0): Launcher control / hidden-apps chooser only visible+openable while Quickstep mode is on; our own icons never in the custom drawer
- [ ] L3 SET1 (consider, then build): curated "Settings" clone — see research/SETTINGS_CLONE_2026-11-02.md
- [x] PWR2 research: can accessibility see the power key / prevent or pre-empt the power menu? (diagnostic in emulator, answer Tj) — ANSWER 2026-11-05: **No.** Android 10 `PhoneWindowManager` handles `KEYCODE_POWER` itself and clears `ACTION_PASS_TO_USER`; `AccessibilityInputFilter` skips events without `FLAG_PASS_TO_USER`, so `onKeyEvent` never gets it (quoted from `android-10.0.0_r47` in `research/BUTTONMAPPER_2026-11-05.md` section 3). Source-based: the emulator diagnostic (`rs3` b0) failed its own volume-up control, so it is NOT measured. The only shell lever is `DISABLE2_GLOBAL_ACTIONS` ("Power menu v2", not built, waits for Tj).

## Tj's v0.2.3 report, 2026-11-02 (verbatim)
"The solitaire opened and the wifi turned off, but the opened apps did not close. The notification access feature says this feature is not available on this device. Maybe it can force stop apps or find another way to close apps, or at least hide the open apps from the recent apps menu. Can the launcher simply make it so it always shows only solitaire in the recent apps menu permanently?"
Findings: killBackgroundProcesses did not close his apps on the tablet; Go devices (low-RAM) do not offer Notification access, so the listener route cannot work there.
- [x] RESET2 (v0.3.0) rebuild the return reset on the ACCESSIBILITY helper (he already enables it): open the notification shade and press "Clear all"; open Recents and press "Clear all" (that removes the tasks AND closes the apps and empties Recents); then go home and open Solitaire; fallbacks: killBackgroundProcesses. Control shows what each step did last time. Drop the notification-access button/status.
- [x] REC1 "always only Solitaire in Recents": system owns Recents; answer + optional auto-clear when Recents opens (accessibility) — decide after RESET2 is tested on the tablet  [DONE or superseded: see STATUS SNAPSHOT]
- [x] REC1 (Tj, 2026-11-02: "If the custom launcher can permanently only show solitaire in the recent apps menu, this is even better than closing the apps") BUILD: every app launched from the custom launcher gets FLAG_ACTIVITY_EXCLUDE_FROM_RECENTS except the target app (Solitaire) => Recents shows only Solitaire, permanently, instantly, no UI automation. Switch in Launcher control (default ON). Caveat: excluded apps keep running (not closeable via Recents Clear all), and are found again via the drawer.  [DONE or superseded: see STATUS SNAPSHOT]
- [x] SW1 (v0.3.0) (Tj, 2026-11-02: "Also add the option to do the clear all button for both the recent apps and the notifications ... put both methods in the launcher so I can see which one works better. I can switch between them in the launcher settings"): three independent switches in Launcher control (Quickstep mode only): (1) Recents shows only Solitaire [launch flag], (2) return reset presses Clear all in the notification shade, (3) return reset presses Clear all in Recents.
- [x] SOL1 (v0.3.0) (Tj, 2026-11-02: "The solitaire opening is a little glitch right now. It was in the recent apps but didn't open on the screen. Diagnose and ship the newest launcher version"): cause = reset pressed HOME then started Solitaire 450 ms later; on the slow tablet HOME lands after Solitaire so the launcher covers it. Fix: no HOME press, start Solitaire last, verify it is in front (accessibility) and re-start up to 3 times; report in Launcher control. Ship v0.3.0 with hide apps + lock + recents policy + switches.

- [x] B1 (v0.3.4: SECONDARY_HOME filter + implicit launch = HOME-type task; emulator checks 'Launcher task is in the HOME-type stack' and 'Recents from the custom home does not list com.tj.launcher'; hedge fixed) launcher must NEVER be listed in Recents: find the best way (hide/ban, or rename "solitaire" + Solitaire icon as fallback)
- [x] B2 (v0.3.4 ScrollView; no JVM test, look at the screenshot) Launcher control menu is too long and does not scroll: make it scroll
- [x] B3 (answered: impractical, Recents is system-owned; apps launched from the custom launcher are already excluded and keep running) research (ignore if impractical): keep apps open but invisible (foreground and Recents) unless Tj does the Quickstep gesture; custom launcher only ever has Solitaire open
- [x] B4 (v0.3.4: skip empty shade (status bar), skip Recents when clean (RecentsDirty, RecentsDirtyTest), black cover, animations off, Solitaire at once when nothing to clear; emulator checks B4) double-Home as fast as possible: 0 animation, only close apps if there are apps, only clear notifications if there are notifications, Solitaire shows IMMEDIATELY and the rest happens invisibly behind it
- [x] B5 folded into C3 (Tj found a different copy; ICS originals sent); was: replace the wallpaper with the NEW attached original leaf wallpaper. BLOCKED: no image arrived with the message (newest upload is the 960x853 one Tj now says is wrong); ask Tj to resend
- [x] B6 (v0.3.4: white touch-absorbing cover over android:id/edit; emulator checks B6) hide or disable the pencil (edit) icon in the notification panel (quick settings)
- B1 design (found in AOSP RecentTasks.isInVisibleRange): an EXCLUDED task is still listed when it is the most recent task (that is why "Launcher" showed when Recents was opened from the home screen); a HOME-type task is never listed. Fix = Launcher gets a MAIN+SECONDARY_HOME intent filter (not the HOME category, so the preferred home does not change) and the router starts it with an IMPLICIT SECONDARY_HOME intent => HOME-type task. Also fixes W5's root cause (the system's "resume home" now finds OUR home task).
- B1 FINDING (emulator run 33, crash-breaker test): a HOME-type Launcher has a cost. Android's AppErrors (Q) calls clearPackagePreferredForHomeActivities when the home process crashes => our default-home setting is cleared and the next Home press shows the "choose a Home app" chooser (the test ended on ResolverActivity). So the HOME-type launch is an OPT-IN switch (Launcher control > Recents, default OFF); without it the card (if it ever shows) is renamed "solitaire" + Solitaire icon. DECISION FOR TJ: default ON (never in Recents; a crash makes Android ask for the Home app again) or OFF (safe; a "solitaire"-looking card can show)?

## Tj's decision, 2026-11-04 (AskUserQuestion answer, verbatim): "Hide completely: on"
- [x] B1 default = ON: the launcher is a HOME-type task (never in Recents). Cost accepted: if the launcher process ever crashes, Android clears our default-home setting and the next Home press shows the "Choose a Home app" dialog. Switch in Launcher control > Recents; "Restore everything" turns it off.

- [x] D6 (v0.3.6; TileProfileTest; emulator 'D6: back in custom mode the tiles are just rotation') per-launcher quick-settings tiles: ONE tile (Auto-rotate) in custom mode, THREE tiles in Quickstep mode. Research answer: YES. The tile list is the secure setting `sysui_qs_tiles`; the minimum-tiles rule is only in SystemUI's tile EDITOR (that is why adb was needed), writing the setting directly bypasses it and SystemUI reloads it live. Needs the WRITE_SECURE_SETTINGS adb grant. Build: write "rotation" on custom, the chosen 3 on Quickstep (snapshot the original first, restore on 'Restore everything'). ASK Tj which three tiles for Quickstep (default suggestion wifi,bt,rotation).

## Found by Claude, 2026-11-05 (emulator runs 125 + 127; Tj did not ask for this, it needs his answer to set the priority)
- [ ] RS1 RETURN RESET WITHOUT NOTIFICATION ACCESS (the real Go tablet's situation): on the emulator with notification access OFF (section `legacy` run ALONE, runs 125 and 127), the double-Home reset's accessibility helper logs `RESET notifications result=1` (PRESSED a "clear all" node in the shade) yet a test notification posted by the shell stays: count before=3 after=3 even after 20 s more. With `base` run first (it grants the listener access with `cmd notification allow_listener`, and nothing ever revokes it) the same check passes (run 128: before=3 after=0; and every gated run). So: (a) the gate has never exercised the no-access path although the check's own comment says it does; (b) the accessibility path may press the WRONG "clear all" (`pressInSystemUi` clicks the FIRST node whose text/description starts with "clear all": Android 10's shade can have a "Silent" section header button AND the footer's button), leaving alerting notifications. The code on this path is byte-identical to v0.7.0 (checked by `git diff`) and **PROVEN pre-existing**: run 129 = section `legacy` alone on the v0.7.0 release commit 2a547f6 gave the identical result (19 PASS, 1 FAIL, the same reset2 check), so v0.7.1 neither introduces nor worsens it. **Ask Tj:** on the tablet, after a double-Home, do the notifications go away? (yes = low priority, the real SystemUI behaves; no = a real bug).
  - [ ] RS1a evidence: run the shade-dump diagnostic on the emulator (open the shade, `uiautomator dump`, list every node whose text or description contains "clear all" with its bounds) before changing anything.
  - [ ] RS1b make the gate honest: `cmd notification disallow_listener` before RESET2 in `legacy` (and a second check with access ON), so both paths are tested in the gate.
  - [ ] RS1c only if RS1a shows two nodes: press the footer's button (the last match) or all matches, prove it with `legacy` alone, then ship as v0.7.2.

- The phone's ADB OTG app is connected to the tablet: prompt `tb8168p1_bsp:/ $` (the MT8168 board name). `id` = uid=2000(shell), gid 2000, groups 2000 shell, 1004 input, 1007 log, 1011 adb, 1015 sdcard_rw, 1028 sdcard_r, 3001 net_bt_admin, 3002 net_bt, 3003 inet, 3006 net_bw_stats, 3009 readproc, 3011 uhid; context u:r:shell:s0. Group `input` means the shell can probably read /dev/input, which the side-key test needs.
- `pm path com.tj.launcher` = `package:/data/app/com.tj.launcher-<random token>/base.apk`: the launcher is installed on the tablet (Android 10 path layout).
- The helper start line was accepted: the shell printed `[1] 10761` (a background job). Its echo looked scrambled in the app (pieces of the line repeated, stray `<` and `>`): it looks like the tablet's shell redrawing a line longer than the screen sideways, which the app does not draw. NOT yet proven that the helper runs: the guide's next card (`ps -A -o ARGS | grep -c '[S]hellServer'`) must print 1.
- [x] PG5 (found by Claude, not asked by Tj) the phone guide's START THE HELPER card should say that a scrambled echo of the long line is normal in the ADB OTG app and that `[1] <number>` is the sign it started, and point at the check card right below it. Needs a PhoneGuide rebuild (v0.7.2): batch it with other guide changes, do not rush it during the cable session. (closed by the 2026-11-06 triage: superseded by phone guide dropped (AF1))
- Second screenshot, 2026-11-05 1:02 on the phone clock: the FIRST helper start attempt left no process (the check printed 0 minutes later); the SECOND attempt printed `[1] 10858`, the check printed 1, then `[1] + Done` (normal: the setsid starter exits after launching the helper). Tj reports the tablet's setup screen still says "Shell helper: NOT running". Facts from the code: that line is computed ONLY when the screen is built (`SetupActivity.build()`, line 157, on resume and after actions), and `ShellHelper.reachable()` gives up after 700 ms (a freshly started helper on this slow tablet may miss it); the helper is reached by the abstract unix socket `tj_shell_helper`, then loopback TCP ports 47631-47635 (INTERNET IS declared in the manifest, so TCP is possible); when it cannot reach the helper the app writes `shell helper not reachable: <reason>` to logcat tag `TjReset`. Diagnostic asked of Tj: that logcat line, the listening check `grep -E 'BA(0F|1[0-3])' /proc/net/tcp` (0A = listening), and if nothing listens a restart that logs the helper's own output to /data/local/tmp/tj.log.
- [x] PG6 (found by Claude) make "the helper did not stay up / the tablet says NOT running" a built-in recovery card in the phone guide: re-open the screen, the logcat reason line, the listening check, the start line that writes its log to /data/local/tmp/tj.log. Also consider making `reachable()` retry for a few seconds instead of one 700 ms try, and adding the last `lastError` to the setup screen's NOT running line. Needs v0.7.2 (with PG5); decide after the real cause is known. (closed by the 2026-11-06 triage: superseded by phone guide dropped (AF1))
- Third batch of screenshots, 2026-11-05 1:10-1:11 on the phone clock (the diagnostics): (a) the launcher's own reason, logged at 13:08:55 and 13:08:57 (tag TjReset): `shell helper not reachable: unix: IOException Connection refused; tcp 47631 ... tcp 47635: SocketException socket failed: EPERM (Operation not permitted)` on EVERY port. (b) `grep -E 'BA(0F|1[0-3])' /proc/net/tcp` printed nothing (exit 1): nothing was listening. (c) the restart-with-log line printed `tj-shell: unix socket up for uid 10243` and `tj-shell: tcp up on 127.0.0.1:47631`: the helper itself starts fine, and the launcher's uid on this tablet is 10243.
- [ ] HLP1 (found by Claude) THE HELPER DIES A FEW MINUTES AFTER IT IS STARTED on this tablet: alive at 1:02 (check printed 1), gone at 13:08 (unix Connection refused), cable and adb session still connected; the first attempt was also gone minutes later. `ShellServer.main` has no exit path of its own (it loops on `tcp.accept()` and the unix accept thread keeps the VM alive), so something outside ends it. Candidates: a vendor memory cleaner, the kernel low-memory killer, the adb session's cgroup, a native crash of the 32-bit ART. Evidence to collect: `cat /data/local/tmp/tj.log` after it dies (a Java crash or exit prints there), `logcat -d -b crash`, and how long it lives (the ps check at the end of the session). The whole "start it once, then unplug" recipe depends on this.
- [x] HLP2 (found by Claude) the launcher cannot create TCP sockets on this tablet: `socket failed: EPERM` on all five ports although `INTERNET` is in the manifest (Android 10 enforces it per UID in netd/BPF: either the permission is not applied to the installed APK's UID on this ROM, or the ROM blocks it). So the TCP fallback in `ShellHelper` is useless here and the abstract unix socket `tj_shell_helper` is the only way in. The report's permission section (`dumpsys package com.tj.launcher`) should show whether INTERNET is granted. (ticked by the 2026-11-06 triage: built and shipped, see BUILDLOG / the item's own STATUS lines)

- [x] CR1 FIRST-OPEN CRASH of the launcher on the real tablet (when exactly and which version is not known yet). Get the stack trace from the tablet report's crash section (`logcat -b crash`; the intake raises a red flag "The crash log has N line(s)") and the "crash breaker tripped" line. If the report has no crash lines (the helper was not running when it was collected, or the buffer rolled over), ask Tj for `logcat -d -b crash | tail -40` from the phone. Do not guess the cause before that: candidates are first-launch initialisation on this ROM or something left over from the previous install. (ticked by the 2026-11-06 triage: built and shipped, see BUILDLOG / the item's own STATUS lines)
- [x] HLP3 (found by Claude) THE LAUNCHER CANNOT REACH A FRESHLY STARTED HELPER on this tablet: the helper printed `unix socket up for uid 10243` and `tcp up on 127.0.0.1:47631` (restart at 1:31 on the phone clock), yet Tj reports "Shell helper: NOT running" on the setup screen right after. Waiting for the launcher's logged reason (tag `TjReset`, last "not reachable" line). Reading the answer: `unix: ... Permission denied` = SELinux blocks the app from the shell's abstract socket (and TCP is EPERM here), so the helper can never be used on this ROM through sockets; fallback to design: a file mailbox in shared storage (the app writes a request file under /sdcard/Android/data/com.tj.launcher/files, the helper polls it and writes the answer; shell and app can both use that folder, the report publish already does). `Connection refused` again = the helper's socket is not visible to the app (different namespace) or it died within a minute. (ticked by the 2026-11-06 triage: built and shipped, see BUILDLOG / the item's own STATUS lines)
- HLP3 ANSWERED (Tj's screenshot, 1:33 on the phone clock, with a LIVE helper): the launcher's log says `shell helper not reachable: unix: IOException Permission denied; tcp 47631: SocketException socket failed: EPERM (Operation not permitted)`. So this tablet's security policy refuses the app's connection to the shell's abstract unix socket (EACCES), and TCP from the app is refused too: NO socket channel between the launcher and the helper works on this ROM. (The launcher's process id changed from 10265 at 13:08 to 12374 at 13:32: it restarted in between, possibly the first-open crash, CR1.) Everything that needs the helper (report probe, "Do everything", task-precise clean-up, keep-alive helper jobs) is unreachable here; the launcher is fail-open, so it degrades instead of breaking.
- [ ] MBX1 (found by Claude) a transport that works without sockets: a FILE MAILBOX in shared storage. The app writes a request file under /sdcard/Android/data/com.tj.launcher/files/mailbox; `ShellServer` (shell) polls it (or uses inotify), runs the SAME allow-listed commands, writes the answer file; the helper accepts a request only if the file's owner uid is the launcher's uid (shell and app can both use that folder: the report publish already relies on it). Needs: client and server code, JVM tests for the protocol, a pref to force the mailbox on the emulator so a scenario section can prove it end to end, then v0.7.2. First confirm on this tablet that the shell can read what the app writes there (ls -l of /sdcard/Android/data/com.tj.launcher/files after the app-side report).
- Fourth screenshot, 2026-11-05 1:37 on the phone clock: the guide's FALLBACK commands run by hand from the phone as ONE line (the helper is unreachable, these run straight in the shell). Result: `dumpsys deviceidle whitelist` printed `Added: com.tj.launcher`; `granted=true` for INTERNET, WRITE_SECURE_SETTINGS, CHANGE_CONFIGURATION, DUMP and READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE (flags USER_SENSITIVE_WHEN_GRANTED|USER_SENSITIVE_WHEN_DENIED|RESTRICTION_INSTALLER_EXEMPT); `enabled_accessibility_services` = `com.tj.launcher/com.tj.launcher.TjAccessibilityService` (the accessibility helper is already on, alone); `cmd package compile -m speed -f` = `Success`. So the permissions, app-ops, battery whitelist and the speed compile are DONE on the real tablet. HLP2 update: INTERNET is `granted=true` at the package level, yet `socket()` from the launcher gives EPERM: the block is not the manifest, so a ROM-level network rule per UID is the likely cause (unexplained).
- CR1 ANSWERED (Tj's 5 screenshots, 1:39-1:40 on the phone clock): `logcat -d -b crash` on the tablet shows THREE crashes of com.tj.launcher at 12:48:47 (pid 9473), 12:48:50 (pid 9548) and 12:48:54 (pid 9643), all `android.app.RemoteServiceException: Bad notification(tag=null, id=7471) posted from package com.tj.launcher, crashing app(uid=10243, pid=...): Couldn't create icon: StatusBarIcon(icon=Icon(typ=RESOURCE pkg=com.tj.launcher id=0x7f07009a) visible user=0 )`. Id 7471 = the keep-alive foreground notification (`KeepAliveService.NOTIFICATION_ID`); 0x7f07009a = `R.drawable.tj_ic_blank` (confirmed in the debug build's R.txt) = an EMPTY vector drawable with no path, used as the invisible small icon (AA2). AOSP's SystemUI (the emulator) accepts it; this tablet's SystemUI cannot create the icon, and Android's NotificationManager then crashes the posting app. Keep-alive has been default ON since v0.7.0, so the very first open of the launcher crash-looped (3 crashes in 7 s) until, probably, the crash guard tripped and switched the feature off (the report's "crash breaker tripped" line will confirm), which is why it works now. The crash guard did its job; the cause is ours (this is the kind of failure L10 is about).
- [x] KA1 (found by Claude, from CR1) fix for v0.7.2: (a) replace the empty vector by a plain transparent PNG small icon (the most conventional format; it cannot be proven on this ROM from the emulator, hence (b)); (b) make keep-alive default OFF until it is proven on this tablet: a feature that can crash-loop the HOME app on first open must be opt-in, and the setup screen / report must say whether it is on; (c) a JVM test that pins "the keep-alive small icon is not an empty vector"; (d) tell Tj in one line and let Tj decide default OFF vs ON (the wish behind it: a faster unlock, AA2). (ticked by the 2026-11-06 triage: built and shipped, see BUILDLOG / the item's own STATUS lines)

- KA1 design refinement (Claude, to build): the bad-notification crash is raised by the SYSTEM (RemoteServiceException on the main thread), so it cannot be caught around startForeground. So besides the PNG icon and default OFF: a FEATURE FUSE in `CrashGuard.record`: if the exception is a RemoteServiceException / its text contains "Bad notification" and our package, switch ONLY keep-alive off (and mark it blocked until Tj re-enables it in Control), do NOT count it toward the 3-crash breaker. One crash then costs one restart, never the custom mode. Pure function + JVM test.
- MBX1 SECURITY FINDING (Claude, not yet decided with Tj): on Android 10 the owner uid of a file under /sdcard/Android/data/<pkg>/ is DERIVED from the folder (the file Tj's shell created showed u0_a243), so "owner uid == launcher uid" is NOT proof that the launcher wrote a request, and other apps with storage permission can write there. So the mailbox must NOT run state-changing commands from a file: only the fixed read-only probe set may be answered that way (state-changing steps stay with the by-hand lines in the phone guide), or the requester must be proven by a channel the system stamps (e.g. `logcat -v uid` tag from the launcher: logd records the real uid; to verify on the emulator). Run `security-review` before shipping MBX1.

ALTERNATIVE "BATCH1": one adb line runs `ShellServer --batch`, which does everything in one go and EXITS: (1) the read-only probe list, (2) the same allow-listed setup actions the buttons run, (3) writes `shell-probe.txt` into /sdcard/Android/data/com.tj.launcher/files/probe/ (+ Downloads); the launcher only READS that file (one direction, harmless). CAUSE (AOSP NotificationManagerService.canUseManagedServices, from memory of the Android 10 source): on a LOW-RAM device (`isLowRamDevice()`; this tablet is a Go device, memory class 128 MB) the system refuses managed services (notification listeners, assistants, condition providers) for every package except those in `config_allowedManagedServicesOnLowRamDevices`, and the shell command skips silently. That is exactly what ReturnReset's own comment says ("Android Go has no Notification access") and why RS1 is the real tablet's situation: the accessibility "Clear all" path is the ONLY notification path here. **MY EARLIER AUDIT LINE WAS WRONG** (CBL1 (a) "MISSING: notification access" and PG7): it cannot be granted on this ROM, nothing is missing there, the guide/Do-everything must NOT offer it. Consequences: the launcher's notification dots can never work on this tablet; RS1 (does the accessibility reset clear the shade on the real tablet? press the footer "Clear all", not a section header) moves up in priority and needs Tj's answer after a double-Home; the emulator gate should test `legacy` (no access) honestly (RS1b).
- CBL1 status after the correction: (a) impossible on this ROM, closed; (b) Home app: Tj did it himself; (d) read-only proof of the grants still wanted; nothing else needs the cable.

KEPT by decision: the first plain retry after a wrong PIN still inherits the gesture (a typo after the gesture is far likelier than a change of mind within 20 s); the LockLog will show it if it ever goes wrong. NOT changed: the reconnect safety net acts only when the keyguard was shown after the session reference and no handshake was confirmed (analysis: with the helper alive throughout it cannot fire).

## Tj, 2026-11-06 ~06:00 UTC (verbatim)
"The actual battery saver tile in the notification panel in quickstep doesn't work"
- [x] AG2 the Battery saver TILE in the notification panel (Quickstep's tile list `qs_tiles_quickstep`, default "wifi,bt,rotation"; Tj has added "battery") does not work in Quickstep. Code facts: the guard (AC3) switches `low_power` off ONLY when `Mode.isCustom`; in real Quickstep it leaves the saver alone; in v0.7.5 (AG1, unreleased) Quickstep switches it ON and keeps it on. So either (a) the mode flag reads custom while Tj is in the stock launcher (the Control line "Battery saver guard: <time> switched battery saver off in the custom launcher (...)" would say so), or (b) Android itself refuses / undoes it (plugged in or charged, first-time confirmation dialog of the tile, sticky flag). ASK Tj for that ONE Control line right after tapping the tile. ADD (v0.7.5, after the review lands): log every `low_power` change the guard sees (old -> new, mode, what it did) into the lock log / report so the cause is never a guess; rerun only `ac`.  [superseded by AZ2 (v0.8.1: the real cause was Android's battery saver state machine going out of step after the launcher's direct writes; the launcher no longer switches the saver ON)]

## Tj, 2026-11-06 ~06:05 UTC (verbatim)
"Also, make it so wifi, Bluetooth, and all notifications are cleared and off whenever the tablet is on the lock screen, from any launcher. The lock screen should never, at any time, show wifi, Bluetooth, or notifications from apps"
- [x] AK1 LOCK SCREEN = NO Wi-Fi, NO Bluetooth, NO app notifications, from ANY launcher (custom or Quickstep), at any time. Today: Wi-Fi/Bluetooth off + notifications cleared only when the CUSTOM launcher becomes active / its radio guard runs (custom mode only); Quickstep keeps whatever is set; the lock screen can show notifications. Design notes written below after reading the code. (ticked by the 2026-11-06 triage: built and shipped, see BUILDLOG / the item's own STATUS lines)
- AK1 DESIGN (Claude, 06:08 UTC; implement as v0.7.6 in a worktree after v0.7.5 ships; app-side only, no helper):
  1. SWITCH `lock_quiet` ("Lock screen: no Wi-Fi, no Bluetooth, no app notifications, in EVERY launcher"), default ON (Tj asked), in Launcher control; second switch `lock_quiet_restore` ("a gesture unlock into Quickstep puts Wi-Fi / Bluetooth back as they were before the lock"), default ON.
  2. NOTIFICATIONS: the app cannot delete other apps' notifications without the helper (no notification access on this Go ROM; `cancelAll` is only its own). What it CAN do: Android's own lock-screen setting `Settings.Secure lock_screen_show_notifications` = 0 (+ `lock_screen_allow_private_notifications` = 0): the lock screen then shows NO app notifications and no notification icons, from any launcher, at every moment, no flicker. Written with WRITE_SECURE_SETTINGS (already granted); the originals go through `RestoreStore.snapshotSecure` so "Restore everything" AND the crash breaker put them back (L10); the switch off restores them too. The notifications themselves stay in the shade until cleared (custom's return reset still clears them; Quickstep keeps them until Tj clears them): TELL Tj plainly. Optional later: also clear the shade at every unlock behind the black cover (flicker; ask first).
  3. RADIOS: at the lock moment (`lockPrepare` when `keyguardLockedFast()`, i.e. +0.7 s / +6.5 s after SCREEN_OFF) remember which radios were on (`lq_prev`), switch Wi-Fi and Bluetooth off with the existing `ReturnReset.radiosOff`; the existing radio receiver (`radioReceiver`) also enforces while the keyguard is locked in ANY mode (`ProfileSession.enforceRadios` gets a `keyguardLocked` input; same 3-per-60-s rate limit); a plain unlock into custom keeps them off (custom rule); a gesture unlock into Quickstep restores them (if `lock_quiet_restore`). Notes: Wi-Fi/Bluetooth toggling by the app works because the app targets an API where `setWifiEnabled` is allowed (the base section proves it on the emulator).
  4. TESTS: JVM for the pure rules (enforce / restore decisions); emulator section `lq` (new, gate-map opt, triggers LockQuiet|ProfileSession|ReturnReset radiosOff): lock -> secure settings 0 and radios off; turn Wi-Fi on while locked -> off again within 3 s; gesture unlock -> restored; plain unlock -> stays off; Restore everything -> settings back; the lock log holds no digit.
  5. RISKS: touches the lock / unlock paths (L10): fail open (any exception = do nothing), never touch the lock method, never block an unlock, rate-limited, respects the breaker pause (`CrashGuard.automationOff`). Do NOT clear notifications by UI automation while the screen is off (unproven).
- AK1 CODED (06:14 UTC) in research/pending/v076-ak1.patch (v0.7.6, NOT applied, v0.7.5 is gating): `LockQuiet` (pure rules + Android side), hooks in `TjAccessibilityService` (lockPrepare -> `onLockAsync`, radio receiver enforces while locked in ANY mode, `applyUnlockChoice` -> `onUnlockAsync` gesture/plain, pref watch + service connect apply the notification setting), `TjApplication`, `RestoreStore.restoreSecure` + `restoreAll` sets the switches false (undo by Restore everything / the breaker), Control (2 switches + status line), report line, emulator section `lq`, gate-map line. PROOF so far: unit 389 / 0 failures (13 new LockQuietTest). NOT yet proven on the emulator (section lq) or on the tablet.

PLAN (v0.7.7, after v0.7.6 ships): AM1-1 new switch `power_card` (default OFF): without it showOverlay / warmUp / hideOverlay / removeOverlay only drive the patch (open brightness while the menu is up, resting brightness otherwise); the card code stays for the emulator tests that exercise it (their feature writers set `power_card` true) and for anyone who wants it; AM1-2 CALIBRATION FROM A REAL SCREENSHOT (AH2): Launcher control button "Calibrate the patch": sets a 4-minute calibration window during which the resting patch is hidden (it must not be in the screenshot); the user locks the tablet, wakes it and takes the screenshot (Power + Volume Down) OR the service asks the system for one (`performGlobalAction(GLOBAL_ACTION_TAKE_SCREENSHOT)`, API 28) once the lock screen has been up for 1.5 s; a MediaStore observer picks the newest screenshot (taken while the keyguard was locked, display-sized, not showing the stock menu), crops the patch window + 24 px and stores it per rotation (`files/patchshot_r<rot>.png` + prefs with wallpaper id, display size, window); the patch then draws THOSE pixels (existing photo path `lockShot` with offsets): resting brightness 1.0, open brightness = the dialog's scrim factor (0.79 default, nudge `patch_scrim_pct`); a changed wallpaper id / display size invalidates it (status line: calibrate again); until calibrated the resting patch stays HIDDEN (no visible block, the flicker stays) and the open patch is the model (profile). AM1-3 prove: JVM tests for the pure rules (pick newest screenshot, validity, crop rectangle, signature), emulator section: screencap while the lock screen shows, the patch at rest vs the bare lock screen over the slot MAE < 0.005. AM1-4 later: measure the scrim from a second capture with the menu open, the same for the home-screen patch.

- AN1 FINDINGS (new session, 2026-11-06 ~08:05Z, code reading; NOT yet proven on the tablet): the helper does its heaviest work exactly when the shade is open in the CUSTOM launcher: `schedulePencil` runs `findPencil()` (getWindows + a view-tree search `findAccessibilityNodeInfosByViewId("android:id/edit")` in SystemUI's window) on EVERY SystemUI accessibility event (leading edge each 40 ms + a debounced one at 90 ms), and `evaluate()` (power-menu text searches over the system windows) does the same each 40 ms / 80 ms while `coverWorld()`. Those searches are executed by SystemUI's own UI thread, the same thread that handles the tile tap: a busy shade = a tile that ignores taps or reacts late. The service config also asks for `flagIncludeNotImportantViews|flagReportViewIds`, `notificationTimeout=0` and no package filter. In QUICKSTEP the pencil search is off (N3) but the guard fights the tile: BatterySaverGuard switches the saver back ON whenever it goes OFF (AG1, reactive, 3 per 10 min) and OFF whenever it goes ON in custom (AC3, reactive, 4 per minute). Android 10's own battery tile is greyed and does nothing while the tablet is PLUGGED IN (BatterySaverTile: STATE_UNAVAILABLE when charging): Tj uses the OTG cable a lot. PLAN: (1) throttle + trailing-debounce the SystemUI-event searches (pencil and power-menu) and COUNT them (AD8 line) so a tablet report shows the real cost; (2) make the guard act only on deliberate transitions (mode change, first start after boot) and keep the old reactive behaviour behind a "strict" switch, default OFF; (3) ask Tj: is the tablet plugged in when the tile does not answer, and does it answer every time when unplugged?

  - AZ1 FINDINGS (Claude, from the Android 10 source, research/AOSP10_BATTERYSAVER_AND_POWERMENU_2026-11-06.md): the darkening is NOT a window dim (the dialog clears FLAG_DIM_BEHIND) but the stock dialog's own `ScrimDrawable` background, alpha = 0.2 * FAST_OUT_SLOW_IN(t/300 ms) (50 % at 105 ms, 78 % at 152 ms, 97 % at 236 ms), colour = the colour extractor's neutral colour. Our patch is an accessibility overlay ABOVE the dialog, so it must reproduce that dim itself. v0.8.0 jumps from brightness 1.0 to DIM_OPEN 0.79 in ONE frame when `evaluateScan` sees the dialog window, so for ~200 ms the patch is DARKER than its still-bright surround (the flicker), and for the ~50-100 ms before the jump it is lighter. Nothing precedes the menu window on Android 10 (NOTES_2026-11-04), so some lag is unavoidable: the fix is to follow the SAME curve from the SAME start (event time) instead of jumping.
  - AZ1 ANSWER to "can the lock screen never darken?": no. The scrim is drawn by the stock dialog inside SystemUI; an accessibility service cannot remove it, and the animator-duration setting only makes it instant (everything else on the lock screen would change too), which would make the lag mismatch worse. A synchronized fade is the right fix.

  - AZ2 ROOT CAUSE (Claude, from the Android 10 source, proven in research/AOSP10_BATTERYSAVER_AND_POWERMENU_2026-11-06.md): `BatterySaverGuard` writes `Settings.Global.low_power` directly (AC3 off-in-custom since v0.7.3, AG1 on-in-Quickstep since v0.7.5). Android 10's `BatterySaverStateMachine` switches the saver on such a write (`setSettingsLocked` -> `enableBatterySaverLocked`) but NEVER updates its own `mState`. A later tap on the tile or on Settings' "Turn off now" goes through `PowerManager.setPowerSaveModeEnabled`, which returns TRUE (so the UI says it worked) while the state machine logs `Tried to disable BS when it's already OFF` / `Tried to enable BS when it's already MANUAL_ON` and does nothing. Only being plugged in or a reboot heals it = "sometimes works". It matches every report since 2026-11-05 22:17 (AC3) including the newest one.

  - [x] AZ2b self-heal: observe `Settings.Secure.low_power_manual_activation_count` (it increments on every accepted "turn ON" request): if it increments and `low_power` is still 0 a moment later, the request was swallowed by a stale state: write `low_power=1` once (this brings saver and state machine back in line: state MANUAL_ON / AUTOMATIC_ON, saver ON). For a saver the OLD versions left stuck ON (state OFF): a one-time clear `low_power=0` at the first start of v0.8.1 when the guard's last action was its own switch-ON.

The bug is REPRODUCED and the cause CONFIRMED by Android's own state-machine dump: BS2 `low_power` written to 1 directly -> `Enabled=true; mState=1` (STATE_OFF) -> Settings "Turn off now" left `low_power=1` (= Tj's "it says it turned off but it never does"). BS3 after the launcher's switch-off the machine reads `Enabled=false; mState=2` (MANUAL_ON, stale) -> the "Turn on now" tap is swallowed -> the self-heal logged `BATSAVER healed` and carried it out -> `Turn off now` works again. (2) CAUSE in v0.8.0 and in the first AZ1 code: the main thread was busy. The first window event of a menu opening ran the lock-number update (`bouncerShown()` = about a dozen node searches in SystemUI's windows) and the power-menu scan (`getWindows()` + node searches in every window) BEFORE the dialog's own event, queued behind them, was handled: event age at handling 80-280 ms with animations on (36-89 ms with animations off). (4) THE EMULATOR: its CI image runs with animations OFF (scrim = one frame); section `pt` now turns the three animation scales to 1.0 for the films and back to 0 after, and measures the surround as the BRIGHTEST of two boxes (the stock panel's shadow dips one box to 70 % for a moment). Even with animations on the emulator's first scrim frames are late (it shows 43 % at its first frame), so the anchor/lead can only be tuned approximately: the sweep fade-25/0/25/50/80 is in `pt`. (5) STILL OPEN: read run after ckpt 537 (S event age should be < 60 ms, patch should dim within ~100 ms of the scrim onset), then choose `PatchFade.LEAD_MS`, tighten the `pt` checks; close handling (patch un-dims when the window is gone; stock scrim fades out over 300 ms with a slow start) only measured, not followed.

- [ ] BA0 READ FIRST (Claude, 2026-11-07): (1) the end level of the open-menu patch is `DIM_OPEN` 0.80 = a pure scalar from the AOSP source (alpha 0.2 of a BLACK scrim) and the emulator (0.795-0.80); v0.8.0 had 0.79, v0.8.1 0.80, and each step made the square fainter ("barely") but never gone, so the real tablet's scrim is NOT exactly 0.8 x black. No measurement of the real scrim exists anywhere in the repo (checked: research/tablet, research/uploads, research/reference/stock: only camera photos, no screenshot pair). `PatchProfile` already records that on this tablet the MODEL patch was 3-25 % too dark with RED most, i.e. its screen darkening has a colour offset the emulator does not have (Android's scrim colour is the colour extractor's neutral colour, which can be a tinted dark colour, not black: out = (1-a)*L + a*C). A scalar can never be 100 % identical for that; a per-channel scale + offset MEASURED ON THE TABLET can.
- [x] BA1 [SHIPPED in v0.8.2, 2026-11-07 02:44 UTC; emulator proof: `pt` phase C runs #272 and #281 (measured look -0.0012 / -0.0024 = invisible); the REAL-TABLET result is tracked by BA4c] PATCH END LEVEL 100 % (Tj: "make the brightness of the square patch match the darkened lock screen after the side key press 100% identical"). Design: the app measures the real scrim ON THE TABLET: bare lock-screen frame S0, then the stock power menu is opened by the helper itself (GLOBAL_ACTION_POWER_DIALOG), a second frame S1 is taken once the stock fade is finished, the menu is closed again (GLOBAL_ACTION_BACK, retried until the window is gone). A robust per-channel fit of S1 = s*S0 + o over the screen OUTSIDE the menu panel / patch / number gives the exact transform; the open patch is then drawn with that transform (ColorMatrix scale + offset) and the fade interpolates identity -> transform (exact for an alpha scrim: both s and o are linear in the scrim alpha). Fail open: any doubt keeps today's 0.80. Once per wallpaper/rotation/size signature (the same moment as the automatic calibration), cap per day, switch in Launcher control (default ON), result + reason in Control and in the tablet report.

- [x] BA2 [SHIPPED in v0.8.2; emulator proof: `bl` run #265 (21 PASS) and final gate #278 {x1,e1,bl,rb}; the real-tablet check is part B of research/TABLET_CHECKLIST_v0.8.2.md] BATTERY SAVER ON THE LOCK SCREEN (Tj: "make the lock screen always have battery saver turned off permanently when the lock screen is up, no matter which launcher was on before the screen lock. If I go to the custom launcher, have the battery saver remain turned off, but if I unlock to quickstep, keep the same battery saver state as the last time I was in quickstep. Only implement these battery saver features if you are confident they can work and not break anything"). Likely reason (Claude's reading): with the saver on, Android turns on the dark theme and may switch animations off, so the lock screen and the stock menu's fade/scrim differ depending on which launcher the lock came from. CONFIDENCE ASSESSMENT before coding (Claude): the only safe writer is `low_power`: OFF is always consistent with Android's state machine (emulator `bs` proved it; a later tile tap that is swallowed is already completed by the AZ2b self-heal). Restoring ON by a direct write is consistent ONLY when Android's state machine still thinks the saver is on (MANUAL_ON / AUTOMATIC_ON, which is what our own OFF write leaves behind); it is NOT consistent after a plug-in or a reboot (the machine goes to OFF: that is the 'it says it turned off but never does' bug). So: restore only when nothing could have reset the machine (not plugged in now, no charging seen since, same boot, `low_power` still 0 and untouched); in any doubt do NOT restore (Tj taps the tile once; the self-heal covers the swallowed tap).

The emulator's own scrim is black x 0.80 (offset 0.00-0.03), so the emulator cannot show the tablet's difference: only the tablet's own measurement can (research/SCRIM_MEASUREMENT_2026-11-07.md says how to read it). Gate runs for the lock/battery-saver paths: #270 id 42321616646 {n,o,t,x,ac,bs,lq}, #271 id 42321619870 {x1,e1,bl,ps,rb} on 5e89113 (jobs 128247261992, 128247271172) were still running at this writing; the independent code review agent was running too.
- BA5 INDEPENDENT REVIEW of BA1/BA2 (agent `ship-code-reviewer`, fresh eyes, 2026-11-07 ~01:25 UTC; read-only brief, 3 major + 5 minor + 4 nits). Dispositions (verified against the code before acting):
  - [x] M1 the Quickstep memory was wiped when a handshake is armed and then abandoned (`Mode.set(QUICKSTEP)` while LOCKED moved `qsStartAt` past the hold): REAL. Fix: only a Quickstep session that begins while the tablet is UNLOCKED counts (`BatterySaverGuard.noteQuickstepBegins` from `Mode.set`, key `bs_qs_real_start`); `holdStillValid` now compares with that.
  - [x] M2 the restore could write low_power=1 after the person's own tile / Settings 'off' in the custom launcher moved Android's state machine to OFF (the AZ2 bug again): REAL. Fix: any change of `low_power` that is not ours and arrives while the tablet is UNLOCKED ends the hold (`onSettingChanged`), so nothing is restored afterwards; the swallowed-tap heal counts as the person's change too.
  - [x] M3 a stray BACK could close the PIN pad / reach the app after an unlock (`scrimCloseStep` pressed the first BACK whenever the helper had opened the menu): REAL. Fix: BACK only when the menu window is verifiably present (`overlayShown` or `dialogWindowPresent(getWindows())`) AND the keyguard is still locked AND no PIN pad; otherwise the helper leaves the menu to the system.
  - [x] m5 a deterministic failure (flat wallpaper, scrim that is not a flat layer) retried every day: REAL. Fix: failures are counted per lock screen signature (`ScrimMeasure.failures/addFailure`, key `scrim_fail_r<rot>`); after 2 the helper stops until the wallpaper / rotation / size / theme changes or 'Measure again'.
  - [x] m6 a frame from the middle of the fade could be stored for good (s 0.90 passed the 0.98 gate): fixed twice: `OPEN_SETTLE_MS` 600 -> 1200 ms and `ScrimFit.MAX_S` 0.98 -> 0.95 (the stock scrim is 0.80).
  - [x] m7 a service teardown mid-measurement left the helper-opened menu up: `scrimTeardown()` from `cancelPatchWork` + `onInterrupt`.
  - [x] m8 three `bl` checks could pass without the unlock having happened: now they also require the `UNLOCKPICK custom` log line (BL2c, BL3b) and the guard's own 'not restored: the tablet was plugged in' line (BL4a).
  - [x] nits: `onUnlock` no longer wipes a hold that began meanwhile; dead local removed; every background thread start is guarded (`async`); `scrimIngesting` / `scrimLastShotId` are volatile.
  - [ ] m4 (accepted, NOT fixed, low risk): nothing checks that no app occludes the keyguard (alarm, secure camera) or that the shade is closed. A guard that tests the active window's type could silently disable the whole measurement on a ROM whose keyguard window looks different, and I cannot test that here; the stage runs once per lock screen, only 3.5 s after a wake, and aborts on screen off / PIN pad / unlock. Revisit if Tj ever reports the menu opening over something.
- AZ4 ROOT CAUSE FOUND (Claude, 2026-11-07 ~01:50 UTC, via the `PS B: DIAG` lines added after gate run #271, reproduced by diag run #275 `bl,ps`): NOT a product bug. An earlier section ends with `setfeat` (lock_quiet back to its default ON, WRITE_SECURE_SETTINGS granted): the app snapshots the ORIGINAL `lock_screen_show_notifications` (1) in `tj_restore.xml` and writes 0. `ps` / `pa` / `pt` then write `lock_quiet=false` in their own feature file: the app RESTORES that stale snapshot (value 1), the lock screen shows notifications again, and their white cards (Android's 'Tap to schedule Battery Saver' suggestion posted after `bl` turned the saver on by hand several times, 'Screenshot saved', 'USB debugging connected') sit over the patch slot: the service's calibration screenshot is refused as 'the power menu was open' (a bright colourless slot) and the resting patch cannot be invisible (= `pa` PA 2 'invisible at rest' MAE 3.4 % in the v0.8.1 long group, and the `ps` B failures of #271). `ps` alone passes because no snapshot exists yet (runs #263, #274, #279). FIX: `ps`, `pa` and `pt` now `rm -f shared_prefs/tj_restore.xml` right after they set the notification setting. [x] AZ4 CONFIRMED: run #280 id 42325739219 `bl,ps,pa` on 2675d47 = success (01:58 UTC).
- FINAL GATE STATE on dced3e9 (Claude, 2026-11-07 02:14 UTC, session branch ccr-823c5a56-yg8voe; it contains everything the old branch claude/power-menu-wallpaper-brightness-g7588s had, nothing stranded): #276 {n,o,t,x,ac,bs,lq} success; #278 {x1,e1,bl,rb} success; #279 `ps` success; #280 `bl,ps,pa` success; #277 {base,v,pa,pt,uf,last} id 42324561598 job 128256727879 was still inside its scenario step after 40 min (a group should take ~13-20; the step limit is 45 min = 02:18 UTC): read its RESULTS block when it ends, then decide (hung emulator vs a real FAIL).

- [ ] BA4 (this is the photo Tj promised; the parent box stays open until v0.8.2 is on the tablet and a new photo / the tablet's own measurement agree)
  - [x] BA4a PHOTOS MEASURED (Claude, 02:14 UTC; scratchpad scripts, to be kept as `tools/patch_photo_step.py`; whole-tablet pair rectified with the `photo_rectify.py` functions on a crop of screen + bezel, threshold 30: aspect 0.580 / 0.582 vs 0.586). Method: inside ONE photo the patch is a hard-edged rectangle on a smooth wallpaper, so a brightness error shows as a step across its edges; line fits on both sides, extrapolated to the edge, the strip beside the card as control (no cross-photo comparison: the two whole-tablet photos have different white balance, R x0.82 / G x0.97 / B x1.14, so a menu-vs-bare ratio is useless). RESULT, all four open-menu photos agree: the patch's bottom edge is 1.04 to 1.10 item pitches below the card (~107 device px: the covered third slot + ~5 px), and the patch is DARKER than the wallpaper right under it by luminance +6.4 (whole photo, level 127), +4.7 / +5.4 / +4.1 (close-ups, levels 62 / 61 / 38) camera units out of 255 (5.0 / 7.6 / 8.8 / 10.9 % of the local level); per channel R +5.0 to +7.3, G +3.6 to +6.4, B +0.3 to +7.0 (blue is clipped by the camera at 244-254 in the whole photo). The control strip beside the card has no step at that row. The patch's LEFT edge gives the same sign (about -4 to -5 units raw in R, G and B).
  - FINDING: the step is nearly the SAME IN ABSOLUTE UNITS at brightness levels that differ 3x (38 vs 127). A scalar dim (what v0.8.0 0.79 and v0.8.1 0.80 are) gives a step proportional to the level, so the tablet's real scrim has an ADDITIVE part (a tinted scrim colour: out = (1-a)*L + a*C, a = 0.2 -> offset 0.2*C of roughly +4..+8 units in red/green here) on top of the 0.80 scale. That is exactly what the v0.8.2 on-tablet fit (scale + offset per channel, `ScrimFit`) is built to measure, and why each scalar step (0.79 -> 0.80) only made the square 'fainter, never gone'. Caveat: camera units (auto exposure and tone curve; the card's white reads ~247, so the gain is near 1), so this settles the direction and rough size only; the helper's own screenshot measurement is exact.
  - [x] BA4b ship v0.8.2 (it contains the fix for exactly this): released 02:44 UTC, see BA3.

If the tablet measures ~0.80 / 0 offset, the step is NOT the scrim: look at calibration freshness / dark theme / a notification card.

Asked Tj which; (a) is far more likely and is explained by a real flaw: `ScrimFit.decode` accepts a stored measurement only for the theme (day / night) it was measured in AND there was ONE storage slot (`patch_scrim_r<rot>`) for both, so every flip of the theme (battery saver forces the dark theme on and off: Tj's tests) made `haveMeasurement` false, the stage measured again (flashes + menu) and overwrote the other theme's value: a flip-flop of up to 4 measurements a day (`MAX_PER_DAY`), and between flips the patch fell back to the generic 0.80.

The value check ('a saver the user turned on stays on 3 s later') passed in all three runs, i.e. the guard never fought the tap. So it is a TEST RACE (counting by `logcat -c` instead of by time), the product is not involved. Attempt 2 (07:15 UTC, `tools/emulator_scenario.sh`): the lines are counted by their OWN epoch stamp (`logcat -d -v epoch` against `date +%s` taken just before the tap) so only a line stamped after the tap counts; INFO `AN1: tap after device epoch ...` prints the counts (after the tap / older ones left behind / lines parsed) and a never-vacuous guard counts every line if the epoch stamps are not recognised. Verification = emulator.yml `base,x1,v,ac` on the pushed head (dispatched 07:16 UTC). LESSON: never count log lines with `logcat -c` + a fixed sleep on this emulator; use time stamps.   - [x] BF1a ANSWER (Claude, 23:45 UTC; given to Tj in chat 01:05 UTC with the Release): YES, it is possible and most of the machinery exists. The "Remove animations" accessibility toggle is nothing but the three `Settings.Global` scales (`window_animation_scale`, `transition_animation_scale`, `animator_duration_scale`) set to 0; the app already holds the adb grant for them (`WRITE_SECURE_SETTINGS`) and `AnimationSpeedup` (Y2b) already writes them for a few seconds during the double-Home reset and puts them back exactly. New: a SESSION-long hold tied to Quickstep, released the same instant the lock screen or the custom launcher is in charge. "Inside Quickstep" is read as the whole Quickstep session (the stock home AND the apps Tj opens from it, because opening an app is where the animation time is); if Tj meant only the stock home screen itself, say so (BF1j).
  - [x] BF1b RULE (built: `AnimationPolicy.why` / `wanted`, `AnimationPolicyTest`; one pure function, the first one was `AnimationPolicy.wanted`): animations are OFF only while ALL of these hold: mode = Quickstep, the lock screen is NOT up (`keyguard` gone), the screen is on, the new switch is on, the adb grant exists, automation is not paused. Everything else = ON (the exact values found before are put back; a value Tj changed by hand meanwhile is left alone). The lock screen counts as the custom world (same idea as `TileProfile.effectiveMode`). Why ON on the lock screen and in custom: the lock-screen patch follows the stock power menu's 300 ms fade (`PatchFade`, v0.8.1 timing "perfect") and needs real animations.
  - [x] BF1c TRIGGERS (built, section `an` proves the screen-off / lock / plain PIN / gesture / double-Home / kill triggers; level-triggered: every trigger re-reads the facts, none trusts the last event): `Mode.set` (any change, incl. every way back to custom), SCREEN_OFF (at once, before the lock screen can show), SCREEN_ON (still locked = stays ON), USER_PRESENT (unlocked in Quickstep = OFF), accessibility service connect, process start (`TjApplication`, also the first start after a boot), "Restore everything", and a cheap re-check on window changes (self-heal). The double-Home reset keeps its own short hold (invisible, behind the cover) and releases it at once when the custom home is in front (no 700 ms tail in a clean start).
  - [x] BF1d SAFETY (built: `AnimationHolds` + `AnimationHoldsTest` 16 tests, `an` AN8 / AN9 / AN9b; the old L10 rules, now for a LONG hold): originals written to disk BEFORE the first change; holds persisted; restored at process start, at boot, at screen off, at every return to custom, by "Restore everything"; a hold that outlives its reason cannot survive the next trigger. Residual risk to state honestly: if the process is killed while a Quickstep hold is on, the scales stay 0 until Android restarts the accessibility service (seconds), which then restores them.

  - [x] BF1f2 CODE REVIEW (Claude, 00:02 UTC; `/code-review high` on 05fece5..HEAD, 6 findings, ALL valid, all fixed in ckpt 640): (1) `reconcileAsync` ended a reset hold 2.5 s after ANY process start, even one that process began itself -> now at once on the worker, skipped when this process began a reset (`resetStartedHere`); (2) a crash loop that dies in under 2.5 s never reached the delayed restore -> the start-of-process look is IMMEDIATE with the helper assumed present (lock screen / screen off / custom / switch off / paused are acted on at once), plus the real helper check at 2.5 s; (3) a stale `pending=true` (a restore that never finished) made the next hold reuse old originals and could overwrite Tj's newer values -> originals re-captured per key (only a scale still at our zero keeps its saved original); the failure rollback only undoes writes when this call took the snapshot; (4) the heartbeat did work in Quickstep even with the switch off -> it checks the switch first and never starts the worker otherwise; (5) `onScreenOn` asked the keyguard twice -> the caller's answer is passed through; (6) SCREEN_ON did the settings writes BEFORE `layerRefresh` / `lockEval` -> posted AFTER them (SCREEN_OFF stays inline: dark screen); plus (7) the reset's 700 ms timer overlapped `settleHome`'s release -> 2 s backstop in a clean start.

Our `HANDOFF.md` is edited only at releases, so EVERY auto-compaction was vetoed (the CLI tried 3 times in 36 s, each failing in ~70 ms with 0 output chars, then its circuit breaker stopped trying, the context grew to the 977K blocking limit, `terminal_reason: blocking_limit`, message "Prompt is too long"). All four recent failed sessions of this project ended at 969K-978K tokens (list price about $30-33 each, about 180M cache-read tokens): `session_01UWi8ZxEmqCcL83UHEaqj9K` 978,004, `...ZzQcUmy1dHAGkxFQdX` 975,725, `...ZvrV7FDzZMv6rWApDX` 975,990, `...Zxcazcaa4QPhF7UPYs` 969,296. NONE ever compacted. It is not the size of any one tool result and not the work: it is a time bomb that fires at the first auto-compaction of any long session. (Tj's own earlier "Compact this session when possible because context window is over 90%", BH2, hit the same wall.)
